=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a circular cake divided into N pieces by cut lines. Each cut line is a line segment connecting the center of the circle to a point on the arc.
The pieces and cut lines are numbered 1, 2, \ldots, N in clockwise order, and piece i has a mass of A_i. Piece 1 is also called piece N + 1.
Cut line i is between pieces i and i + 1, and they are arranged clockwise in this order: piece 1, cut line 1, piece 2, cut line 2, \ldots, piece N, cut line N.
We want to divide this cake among K people under the following conditions. Let w_i be the sum of the masses of the pieces received by the i-th person.

- Each person receives one or more consecutive pieces.
- There are no pieces that no one receives.
- Under the above two conditions, \min(w_1, w_2, \ldots, w_K) is maximized.

Find the value of \min(w_1, w_2, \ldots, w_K) in a division that satisfies the conditions, and the number of cut lines that are never cut in the divisions that satisfy the conditions. Here, cut line i is considered cut if pieces i and i + 1 are given to different people.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Let x be the value of \min(w_1, w_2, \ldots, w_K) in a division that satisfies the conditions, and y be the number of cut lines that are never cut. Print x and y in this order, separated by a space.

Constraints


- 2 \leq K \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 10^4
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 2
3 6 8 6 4

Sample Output 1

13 1

The following divisions satisfy the conditions:

- Give pieces 2, 3 to one person and pieces 4, 5, 1 to the other. Pieces 2, 3 have a total mass of 14, and pieces 4, 5, 1 have a total mass of 13.
- Give pieces 3, 4 to one person and pieces 5, 1, 2 to the other. Pieces 3, 4 have a total mass of 14, and pieces 5, 1, 2 have a total mass of 13.

The value of \min(w_1, w_2) in divisions satisfying the conditions is 13, and there is one cut line that is not cut in either division: cut line 5.

Sample Input 2

6 3
4 7 11 3 9 2

Sample Output 2

11 1

Sample Input 3

10 3
2 9 8 1 7 9 1 3 5 8

Sample Output 3

17 4

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's understand counting.

We need count cut lines that are never cut in any optimal division. A cut line c (between c and c+1 mod N) is never cut if in every optimal partition, pieces c and c+1 belong to same person. Equivalently no optimal partition has a boundary at c. Since exactly K boundaries cut in each optimal partition. We need count positions c in 0..N-1 such that boundary at c is not used in any optimal partition.

How to determine? For optimal X, consider set of possible cut positions in optimal partitions. A cut line c is cut in some optimal partition iff there exists a partition into K arcs each sum >= X with a boundary at c. Since X is max, any partition with min >= X is optimal (because min cannot exceed X; if min >= X and X is maximum, min = X). So "divisions satisfying conditions" = partitions into K arcs each sum >= X. Thus y = number of cut lines c such that no feasible partition with threshold X has a boundary at c. Equivalently for every feasible partition, c is internal.

So we need count positions that are not a boundary in any feasible K-partition with each segment sum >= X.

Maybe can characterize boundary positions using "forced cuts"? Actually if a cut line is never cut, it lies inside a segment in every feasible partition. Since all A_i positive, maybe these are pieces that are "too small" to be separated? Need compute.

Let's formulate feasible partitions.

Given threshold X, define a directed graph on cut positions (0..N-1) maybe. A partition into K arcs corresponds to choosing K cut positions (boundaries) b_0 < b_1 < ... < b_{K-1} along doubled positions, with b_K = b_0+N, such that each arc sum from b_t to b_{t+1} >= X. Since arcs are consecutive pieces: if boundary positions are cut lines, segment from b_t to b_{t+1} (exclusive) includes pieces b_t, ..., b_{t+1}-1 (mod N) and sum >= X. Need b_t distinct modulo N, K boundaries.

Thus feasible partitions are K-cycles in a directed graph on positions 0..2N? Edge u->v if u < v <= u+N and sum A_u..A_{v-1} >= X. Need a cycle of length K around N positions.

Because all weights positive, for each start u, the set of possible next boundary v is an interval [f(u), u+N]? Wait f(u) is minimal v with sum>=X. Any v >= f(u) and v <= u+N? Actually if v > u+N, arc would cover more than full circle, not allowed for a segment in partition? Boundaries around circle: each segment length < N pieces? Since K>=2, each person gets one or more consecutive pieces, but could a person receive all N? No K>=2, each nonempty, so each segment length <= N-K+1 < N. Thus v < u+N. Also sum of all N pieces S >= KX >=2X, so u+N maybe sum>=X, but segment cannot be full circle. For partition, each arc should not include all pieces; but if v = u+N, then segment is all N pieces, leaving no pieces for others, impossible for K>=2. However in our greedy feasibility we allowed e = s+N after K segments? Wait if K segments, the last segment ends at s+N, so no segment individually has v=s+N except maybe if K=1? Can we check feasibility and count y without binary lifting using a different method O(N) per check? Let's explore.

We need for each t in 1..N, determine if f^K(t) <= t+N. This is a "K-th successor within limit". There might be an O(N) algorithm using "doubling with two pointers" for a fixed K? Since f is monotone, maybe we can compute f^K(t) for all t in O(N) using "sliding window of K jumps" or "parallel binary search"? Let's investigate.

Given f nondecreasing and f(t)>t. We want h(t)=f^K(t). Because f is nondecreasing, h(t) is nondecreasing. We can compute h(t) for all t by simulating K rounds? For round r, compute f^r(t) for all t. We have a functional graph where f(i)>i. We need for each node i, the K-th successor. This is a classic "level ancestor" problem. Level ancestor in a tree: Given a rooted tree (parent pointers to INF), answer ancestor at distance K. There is a well-known O(N) preprocessing, O(1) query using ladder decomposition (Bender-Farach). But implementing for each check might be heavy but possible? However our graph is not a tree? Each node has one parent f(i), but multiple children. It is a forest of rooted trees with edges child -> parent (parent > child). We need K-th ancestor for N nodes. Let's see if we can implement simpler O(N) for all queries with same K using "jump pointers" and "long path".

Level ancestor with ladder decomposition:
- For each tree, compute depth (distance to root INF).
- Decompose into long paths: For each node, choose child with maximum height (deepest) as "heavy" child, forming paths. For each path, store nodes from top to bottom and also extend upward by path length to allow jumps.
- To answer ancestor at distance d from node v, if d <= depth[v], climb to top of v's path? Standard:
  - Precompute for each node its path id and index in path.
  - For query (v, d), if d <= index[v] (distance from top of path), answer path[index[v]-d].
  - Else need go to parent of top and continue with d - (index[v]+1). This may take multiple path jumps. To make O(1), each path stores extra ancestors above top, and we can jump to ancestor at depth depth[v]-d using the path that contains that ancestor? Actually ladder decomposition: For each node, store the ladder (path extended upward by its length). Then query can be answered by jumping to the top of the ladder? Let's recall.

def check(X):
    # compute f
    f = [INF] * M
    r = 1
    pref_local = pref
    for t in range(M-1):  # 0..2N
        if r < t+1: r = t+1
        while r <= N2 and pref_local[r] - pref_local[t] < X:
            r += 1
        if r <= N2:
            f[t] = r
    f[INF] = INF
    # build children
    head = [-1] * M
    to = [0] * (M-1)  # edges for nodes 0..2N (M-1 nodes)
    nxt = [0] * (M-1)
    idx = 0
    for i in range(M-1):  # 0..2N
        p = f[i]
        to[idx] = i
        nxt[idx] = head[p]
        head[p] = idx
        idx += 1

    cnt_infeasible = 0
    stack = [(INF, 0)]? Need avoid tuple overhead? M=400k, tuple stack okay but maybe optimize.

Pseudo:

def count_infeasible(X):
    N2 = 2*N
    INF = N2 + 1
    M = INF + 1  # N2+2
    f = [INF] * M
    pref_local = pref
    r = 1
    # compute f for t=0..N2
    for t in range(N2 + 1):  # 0..2N
        if r < t + 1:
            r = t + 1
        # while r <= N2 and pref[r] - pref[t] < X
        # Use local variables for speed
        while r <= N2 and pref_local[r] - pref_local[t] < X:
            r += 1
        if r <= N2:
            f[t] = r
    f[INF] = INF
    # Build children
    head = [-1] * M
    to = [0] * N2  # nodes 0..N2-1? Wait nodes 0..2N inclusive count N2+1. M-1 = N2+1? Let's compute: N2=2N. Nodes 0..N2 inclusive count N2+1 = 2N+1. INF=N2+1. M=N2+2. Edges from nodes 0..N2 to parent: N2+1 edges. So to length N2+1.
    nxt = [0] * (N2 + 1)
    idx = 0
    for i in range(N2 + 1):  # 0..2N
        p = f[i]
        to[idx] = i
        nxt[idx] = head[p]
        head[p] = idx
        idx += 1
    # DFS
    cnt = 0
    stack = [(INF, 0)]
    path = []
    while stack:
        v, state = stack.pop()
        if state:
            path.pop()
        else:
            path.append(v)
            depth = len(path) - 1
            if 1 <= v <= N:
                if depth < K:
                    cnt += 1
                else:
                    anc = path[depth - K]
                    if anc > v + N:
                        cnt += 1
            stack.append((v, 1))
            e = head[v]
            while e != -1:
                stack.append((to[e], 0))
                e = nxt[e]
    return cnt

Wait if start earlier, the sum to b2 is original second segment sum + extra pieces >= X, so the minimal endpoint g2 is <= b2. Yes. So greedy endpoints are <= original boundaries. Thus if original fits, greedy fits. Good.

Now, let's refine check function for speed.

Implementation details:

```
import sys

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,K = map(int,input().split())
    A = list(map(int,input().split()))
    S = sum(A)
    N2 = 2*N
    INF = N2 + 1
    M = INF + 1  # N2+2
    pref = [0]*(N2+1)
    for i in range(N2):
        pref[i+1] = pref[i] + A[i % N]
    
    def count_infeasible(X):
        f = [INF] * M
        r = 1
        pref_local = pref
        N2_local = N2
        X_local = X
        # compute f
        for t in range(N2_local + 1):
            if r < t + 1:
                r = t + 1
            while r <= N2_local and pref_local[r] - pref_local[t] < X_local:
                r += 1
            if r <= N2_local:
                f[t] = r
        # f[INF] already INF
        head = [-1] * M
        to = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        nxt = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        idx = 0
        for i in range(N2_local + 1):
            p = f[i]
            to[idx] = i
            nxt[idx] = head[p]
            head[p] = idx
            idx += 1
        cnt = 0
        stack = [INF]
        path = []
        K_local = K
        N_local = N
        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            if v < 0:
                path.pop()
                continue
            path.append(v)
            depth = len(path) - 1
            if 1 <= v <= N_local:
                if depth < K_local:
                    cnt += 1
                else:
                    anc = path[depth - K_local]
                    if anc > v + N_local:
                        cnt += 1
            stack.append(~v)
            e = head[v]
            while e != -1:
                stack.append(to[e])
                e = nxt[e]
        return cnt
```

Wait, `~v` for v=0 is -1, for v=INF is -INF-1. When we pop negative, we don't need decode. But path.pop() should pop the node that was entered. Since exit marker is pushed immediately after entering v, and children are pushed after, children are processed before exit marker. However, what about siblings? Suppose v has children c1,c2. We push ~v, then push c1, c2. Stack top c2, process c2 and its subtree, then c1, then ~v. Good. Path after processing c2 returns to v. Then c1. Then exit v. Good.
But note: We push children by iterating linked list head[v]. The order is reversed but irrelevant. Good.

Potential bug: When we push `~v`, if v=0, marker=-1. If later we pop -1, we do path.pop(). But what if a legitimate node index is -1? None. Good.

Now, in f computation, for t=N2, r maybe N2+1, f[N2]=INF. But we still add edge N2->INF. Good.
For t=0, if f[0]=0? No, f[0]>=1. Good.

Potential micro-optimizations:
- In f computation, `while r <= N2_local and pref_local[r] - pref_local[t] < X_local: r += 1`. This condition accesses pref_local[t] each while iteration. Since pref_local[t] constant, compute `pt = pref_local[t]` before while. Then while `pref_local[r] - pt < X_local`. This is faster.
```
for t in range(N2_local + 1):
    if r < t + 1: r = t + 1
    pt = pref_local[t]
    while r <= N2_local and pref_local[r] - pt < X_local:
        r += 1
    if r <= N2_local:
        f[t] = r
```
Good.
- In children build, `for i in range(N2_local + 1): p = f[i]` etc.
- In DFS, `head_local=head; to_local=to; nxt_local=nxt; stack=[INF]; path=[]; cnt=0; K_local=K; N_local=N`.
- Use `append = stack.append`? But stack changes? Could localize `stack_append = stack.append`, `stack_pop = stack.pop` but after list grows, methods remain valid. However, if we use `stack_append` and `stack_pop`, it's faster. But we also need `stack` itself. We can do:
```
stack = [INF]
while stack:
    v = stack.pop()
...
```
Fine.

Maybe use `path_append = path.append`, `path_pop = path.pop`? But path list same. Could localize:
```
path_append = path.append
path_pop = path.pop
...

...
path_pop()
```
But path_append remains valid. Good.
Similarly `stack_append = stack.append`, `stack_pop = stack.pop`.
However, if we use `stack_append` and `stack_pop`, the while condition `while stack:` uses stack. Fine.

Let's implement DFS with local aliases:
```
stack = [INF]
path = []
cnt = 0
while stack:
    v = stack.pop()
    if v < 0:
        path.pop()
        continue
    path.append(v)
    depth = len(path) - 1
    if 1 <= v <= N_local:
        if depth < K_local:
            cnt += 1
        else:
            anc = path[depth - K_local]
            if anc > v + N_local:
                cnt += 1
    stack.append(~v)
    e = head[v]
    while e != -1:
        stack.append(to[e])
        e = nxt[e]
```
This is okay. `path.pop()` and `path.append` are method lookups each time; okay.

Could use `if v <= N_local and v >= 1` but `1 <= v <= N_local` is fine.

Now, let's think about if the stack could contain a negative marker and a child with value -1? no.

```
AA = A * 2
pref = [0] * (2*N + 1)
for i in range(2*N):
    pref[i+1] = pref[i] + AA[i]
```
This is O(N). Good.

Let's test with a potential counterexample: Original partition boundaries t=0? Suppose first segment is very long, causing greedy second segment to start earlier and maybe need more than K segments? Wait greedy endpoints are <= original boundaries, so if original has K segments ending at t+N, greedy K-th endpoint <= t+N. Let's prove by induction:
Let original boundaries b_0=t, b_1,...,b_K=t+N. g_0=t. Assume g_r <= b_r. Then g_{r+1}=f(g_r). Since f is nondecreasing, f(g_r) <= f(b_r). Now b_{r+1} is an endpoint from b_r with sum>=X, so f(b_r) <= b_{r+1}. Thus g_{r+1} <= b_{r+1}. Base g_0=b_0. So g_K <= b_K=t+N. Good. This requires f(b_r) defined; b_r<=t+N<=2N for r<=K? b_K=t+N<=2N, f(b_K) not needed for r=K? For r=K-1, f(b_{K-1}) <= b_K, and b_{K-1}<b_K<=2N, so f(b_{K-1}) exists <=2N. Good. Let's write the final code carefully:

```
import sys
import gc

def solve():
    gc.disable()
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    S = sum(A)
    N2 = 2 * N
    INF = N2 + 1
    M = INF + 1

    AA = A * 2
    pref = [0] * (N2 + 2)
    for i in range(N2):
        pref[i+1] = pref[i] + AA[i]
    pref[N2+1] = 10**30

    def count_infeasible(X):
        f = [INF] * M
        r = 1
        pref_local = pref
        N2_local = N2
        X_local = X
        for t in range(N2_local + 1):
            if r < t + 1:
                r = t + 1
            pt = pref_local[t]
            while pref_local[r] - pt < X_local:
                r += 1
            if r <= N2_local:
                f[t] = r
        # f[INF] remains INF

        head = [-1] * M
        to = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        nxt = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        idx = 0
        for i in range(N2_local + 1):
            p = f[i]
            to[idx] = i
            nxt[idx] = head[p]
            head[p] = idx
            idx += 1

        cnt = 0
        stack = [INF]
        path = []
        K_local = K
        N_local = N
        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            if v < 0:
                path.pop()
                continue
            path.append(v)
            depth = len(path) - 1
            if 1 <= v <= N_local:
                if depth < K_local:
                    cnt += 1
                else:
                    anc = path[depth - K_local]
                    if anc > v + N_local:
                        cnt += 1
            stack.append(~v)
            e = head[v]
            while e != -1:
                stack.append(to[e])
                e = nxt[e]
        return cnt

    low = 0
    high = S // K + 1
    while high - low > 1:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        if count_infeasible(mid) < N:
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid
    x = low
    y = count_infeasible(x)
    print(x, y)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```

For t=N2-1, r might be N2+1 from earlier if no endpoint for previous t. Then while sentinel - pref[N2-1] >=X, false, f=INF. Good.

Full code:

```
import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    K = data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    del data

    S = sum(A)
    N2 = 2 * N
    INF = N2 + 1
    M = INF + 1

    AA = A * 2
    pref = [0] * (N2 + 2)
    for i in range(N2):
        pref[i+1] = pref[i] + AA[i]
    pref[N2+1] = 10**30
    del AA

    def count_infeasible(X):
        f = [INF] * M
        r = 1
        pref_local = pref
        N2_local = N2
        X_local = X
        for t in range(N2_local + 1):
            if r < t + 1:
                r = t + 1
            pt = pref_local[t]
            while pref_local[r] - pt < X_local:
                r += 1
            if r <= N2_local:
                f[t] = r

        head = [-1] * M
        to = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        nxt = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        idx = 0
        for i in range(N2_local + 1):
            p = f[i]
            to[idx] = i
            nxt[idx] = head[p]
            head[p] = idx
            idx += 1

        cnt = 0
        stack = [INF]
        path = []
        K_local = K
        N_local = N
        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            if v < 0:
                path.pop()
                continue
            path.append(v)
            depth = len(path) - 1
            if 1 <= v <= N_local:
                if depth < K_local:
                    cnt += 1
                else:
                    anc = path[depth - K_local]
                    if anc > v + N_local:
                        cnt += 1
            stack.append(~v)
            e = head[v]
            while e != -1:
                stack.append(to[e])
                e = nxt[e]
        return cnt

    low = min(A) - 1
    high = S // K + 1
    while high - low > 1:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        if count_infeasible(mid) < N:
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid

    x = low
    y = count_infeasible(x)
    print(x, y)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    K = data[1]
    A = data[2:2 + N]
    del data

    S = sum(A)
    N2 = 2 * N
    INF = N2 + 1
    M = INF + 1

    AA = A * 2
    pref = [0] * (N2 + 2)
    for i in range(N2):
        pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + AA[i]
    pref[N2 + 1] = 10 ** 30
    del AA

    def count_infeasible(X):
        f = [INF] * M
        r = 1
        pref_local = pref
        N2_local = N2
        X_local = X

        for t in range(N2_local + 1):
            if r < t + 1:
                r = t + 1
            pt = pref_local[t]
            while pref_local[r] - pt < X_local:
                r += 1
            if r <= N2_local:
                f[t] = r

        head = [-1] * M
        to = [0] * (N2_local + 1)
        nxt = [0] * (N2_local + 1)

        idx = 0
        for i in range(N2_local + 1):
            p = f[i]
            to[idx] = i
            nxt[idx] = head[p]
            head[p] = idx
            idx += 1

        cnt = 0
        stack = [INF]
        path = []
        K_local = K
        N_local = N

        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            if v < 0:
                path.pop()
                continue

            path.append(v)
            depth = len(path) - 1

            if 1 <= v <= N_local:
                if depth < K_local:
                    cnt += 1
                else:
                    anc = path[depth - K_local]
                    if anc > v + N_local:
                        cnt += 1

            stack.append(~v)
            e = head[v]
            while e != -1:
                stack.append(to[e])
                e = nxt[e]

        return cnt

    low = min(A) - 1
    high = S // K + 1

    while high - low > 1:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        if count_infeasible(mid) < N:
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid

    x = low
    y = count_infeasible(x)
    print(x, y)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
